# AGNOS — GPU

> **The single GPU document.** It replaced six planning files — the 1.54.x compute arc, the 1.55.x display
> arc, the 1.56.x shader arc, the HDMI-modeset investigation, an earlier HDMI-audio plan, and a stale
> session handoff in agnosticos. All seven are deleted; everything in them worth keeping is below.
>
> ⚠ **Why this file exists is a failure worth naming, once, at the top.** GPU was declared DONE when it was
> not. The remainder then came back as three more arcs — 1.55.x "display", 1.56.x "shader", and an
> HDMI-audio investigation — and a draft was about to sell a fourth ("1.57.x modeset") plus a fifth
> (`gfx3d-arc.md`). **That is one unfinished job wearing six names, and six documents to track it in.**
>
> **Rules for this file, so it does not happen again:**
> 1. **No new GPU arc documents.** Everything GPU goes here.
> 2. **No cryptic bite IDs.** The old ones — `C2g-1`, `S0`-`S12`, `D0`-`D2`, `P4`, `A4`, `M1`-`M9` — are
>    unreadable six weeks later and made the same work look like new work each time it was renamed. Items
>    below are named for **what they deliver**.
> 3. **Everything maps to a release.** No item exists without a version it ships in.
> 4. **GPU is not "done" until the release plan below is finished.** Not a phase of it.

---

## Where it stands

| Shipped | What agnos gained | Release |
|---|---|---|
| Compute | Firmware load via PSP · engine start · queue · PM4 ring · hand-assembled gfx90c shaders · integer + f64 matmul, rosnet-bit-correct from ring 3 | 1.54.x |
| Display | Read the live pipe · scanout flip · vblank pacing · console geometry fix · hardware 2D via CP-DMA · ring-3 band `#84`-`#89` | 1.55.x |
| Shader compositing | Per-pixel alpha · 2-D grids · EXEC bounds guard · glyph expand · coverage · gradient · one-submission batched frame (1.78x, pixel-identical) · the `#92` descriptor ABI | 1.56.x |
| Screen-capture + blit | `#90 gpu_readback_shm` (reuse-safe via a `clflush` on the readback window) + `#91 gpu_blit_bb` (overlap-safe bb→bb) — the `#89→#92` numbering hole closed | 1.56.x |
| ML on the cores | real attn11 / rupantara / tentib layer matmuls execute on gfx90c, **bit-identical vs CPU** — THREE proven consumers (f64 via `#83` ×2 + ternary integer via `#82`); the 1.54.x crown, closed | 1.56.x |
| **Modeset** | **own the live pipe from ring 3** — `#93 gpu_modeset_op`, ops 0x00-0x06. Read the pipe (M1/M2/M3) · OTG lock (M4) · VTOTAL retime (M5, refresh 59.951→59.152→59.951 Hz to the milliHz) · **the OTG envelope (M6): `OTG_MASTER_EN` 0→1, frame counter 2615→0→115, panel blanked and relit** · DIG/BE (M8) · the in-boot sequencing experiment (M9) | 1.56.9-1.56.15 |
| **3D — a rasteriser** | **agnos renders textured triangles on its own GPU.** Edge coverage (rung 9) · the crossover kill-gate measured, GPU wins at 1751 px (10) · barycentric RGBA (11) · tri-list (12) · affine texturing (13) · fused `TEX_LIST` + col-major (14/14b) · **integer 4-tap bilinear (15)** · tile-ownership proof (16). Ops `0x08`-`0x0C`; **18** hand-assembled shaders. ✅ Rung 15 **fully iron-validated** (blob, ABI, residency, dispatch, 48-VGPR descriptor **and** the filter kernel) — the first burn exposed a half-texel offset, fixed in-cycle and confirmed by the re-burn | 1.56.16-1.56.29 |

**What agnos still cannot do:** set a resolution from cold on a pipe the firmware never lit · drive a second
monitor · get audio out over HDMI · render with bilinear filtering, depth, or perspective correction.

⚠ **Two entries were removed from that list on 2026-07-28, having been false for weeks.** "Render anything in
3D" — rungs 9-14b are iron-closed and textured triangles are byte-identical to the host reference. "Put a
picture on a display the firmware did not already light" is now **half** true: agnos owns and re-times the
*live* pipe (M1-M6 above), so what remains is specifically the **cold** case. The gap between this table and
reality is the failure mode this document exists to prevent — see the release plan's ⛔ note.

## The release plan

# **1.56.x — GPU. All of it. One release.**

⚠ **There is no 1.57/1.58/1.59/1.60.** A draft once invented four minor versions and scattered the remaining
GPU work across them — the exact fragmenting this document exists to stop. **GPU is ONE release, already
open.** Patch increments cut as work lands (…1.56.6, 1.56.7, 1.56.8); the minor closes when the last
REMAINING item is done. Order below is forced by dependencies, not preference.

⛔ **A release item is DONE when its consumer can use it, not when the kernel half compiles.** That is the
lesson of 1.54.x's C6 and 1.56.x's `#92` — the same mistake twice — and it is why the DONE list is a list of
things with callers, not a list of things that build.

### ✅ DONE — shipped and verified

| # | What | Landed |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`#92` / the GPU display band has consumers.** Root blocker was memory, not alpha: setu allocated client buffers as `shm_create` **#71 = system RAM** the GPU cannot reach, so the kernel rejected every slot. **setu 0.6.0** asks `shm_create_gpu` **#86** first. **aethersafha** then composites on the GPU — chrome via `blit`#39 DEFERRED, opaque surfaces via **#87**, translucent ones via **#92** op 0x01 (premultiplied src-over), one **#84** flip. Full premultiplied chain: sadish `sd_premul` → setu `SETU_SURF_PREMULTIPLIED` → aethersafha. | 10 repos released on cyrius 6.4.71: setu 0.7.0 · aethersafha 0.9.7 · sadish 0.5.0 · dhancha 0.9.2 · rekha 0.3.3 · rupa 0.1.1 · bhumi 1.1.1 · crab 0.4.2 · jalwa 1.4.2 · puka 0.6.6 |
| **2** | **Shader ISA is sovereign and byte-verified reference exists.** The four hand-typed 1.54.x matmul kernels gained `.s` sources proven byte-identical to their committed hex; the committed hex **is** the iron-proven authority. ⚠ **The llvm-mc build gate was REMOVED at 1.56.8** — llvm-mc is C++, and a sovereign build depends on no C/C++ toolchain. The sovereign gfx90c assembler is mabda's Cyrius `gfx9_encode.cyr`. | 1.56.6 → 1.56.8 |
| **3** | **The two 1.56.3 re-audit defects** — verified already fixed at 1.56.4 (coverage-dispatcher arity green; shader slots at `0x50000`, clear of the sink page). No change needed. | verified 1.56.6 |
| **4** | **D-1 / D-2 / D-4 ratified.** D-1: llvm-mc rejected (C++, out). D-2: `#92` blend = premultiplied f32, frozen (consumed end-to-end; straight-alpha `rend_blend` is dead code). D-4: whole-surface translucency IS a real MUDRA/SHANTA requirement → the DCN second plane stays a live deferred item. | 1.56.8 |
| **5** | **`#90 gpu_readback_shm` + `#91 gpu_blit_bb`** — the two reserved CP-DMA jobs, now implemented, iron-proven and reuse-safe; the `#89→#92` numbering hole is closed. **`#90`** GPU-copies a rect OUT of the back buffer into a client shm slot (screen capture); **`#91`** copies a rect within the back buffer (window move / scroll — overlap-safe: downward moves copy bottom-up). ⚠ **A real readback-coherence bug was found + fixed here:** reading into a REUSED shm slot returned a stale cache-line ghost (gpu.md C2g-1 class), so `gpu_readback_shm_sys` now `clflush`es the destination window `[shm_kva, +row*h)` before the CP-DMA (a new disassembly-verified `clflush(addr)` primitive in `io.cyr`). Proven by **`/bin/gpucopy`** (banded-source + overlapping-move oracle) → `run: exit 95` on archaemenid — 5 burns, 84→76→51→95→95, each with a CONFIRM/FALSIFY rubric. Cyrius wrappers filed + iron-ready ([`issues/2026-07-23-gpu-readback-blit-bb-wrappers.md`](../issues/2026-07-23-gpu-readback-blit-bb-wrappers.md)). | 1.56.8 · iron 2026-07-23 |
| **6** | **ML on the shader cores — the 1.54.x crown, CLOSED.** A real **rupantara** MLP up-projection matmul (`linear_fwd 8×8×32`, bias-free) executes on the gfx90c shader cores, **bit-identical** vs rosnet's production CPU `linear_fwd` on fractional f64 data. New **`rupantara/src/gpu.cyr` `linear_fwd_gpu`** tiles the matmul into 8×8×8 blocks and dispatches each via **`#83 gpu_dispatch_f64`** (iron-proven since 1.54.x), one tile per call, gathering strided sub-blocks CPU-side; bit-exact because both `#83` (unfused `v_mul_f64`+`v_add_f64`) and `linear_fwd` (cyrius `EMIT_F64V_FMADD` = SSE2 `mulpd+addpd`, no f64 hardware FMA) round identically, and K=8 is a single k-tile. Proven by **`/bin/gpulayer`** → `run: exit 95` on archaemenid (host tiling proof exit 96 → one iron burn). **Retires the "GPU capability with no caller" anti-pattern this item named** — THREE proven callers ("three jewels", all iron exit 95, 2026-07-23): **rupantara 0.4.1** (f64 up-projection via `#83`, bit-exact K≤8, `/bin/gpulayer`), **tentib 1.0.1** (ternary integer via `#82`, `/bin/gpumm`, **bit-exact at any K** since integer accumulate is associative — proven K=16 cross-tile; also the first negative-integer proof of `#82`), and **attn11 1.14.1** (the parent transformer's own `qlinear_fwd` hook routed to `#83`, `/bin/gpuattn`). ⚠ f64 K>8 (down-projection, cross-tile) is a ~1 ULP follow-on; the integer path has no such limit. Follow-on: wire the offload into the full multi-layer forward per repo. | rupantara 0.4.1 + tentib 1.0.1 + attn11 1.14.1 · iron 2026-07-23 |

### ▶ REMAINING — as numbered 1.56.x cuts

⛔ **This table was rebuilt 2026-07-28 against the kernel source after a full review, because the previous
version was materially false.** It listed MODESET as "not started" when item 7's own closing criterion had
been met on iron six weeks of patches earlier (1.56.12); it gated 3D on MODESET when 3D had already shipped
rungs 9-14b *without* it; and it prescribed a zero-burn HDMI-audio discriminator (L1) that had already run
and returned VOID. **Three agents in a row planned against those rows.** The lesson is written into rule 4 at
the top of this file: a REMAINING row that has not been re-derived from source is a liability, not a plan.

Every row below is a **patch cut**. Order is forced by dependency and by burn cost — the two no-burn cuts
come first deliberately, so the ledger gets true before anything else is built on it.

| Cut | Deliverable | Burns | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|

both are correct under WRAP and wrong under CLAMP, the third such instance in this rung. ✅ **The `gputex` BILINEAR arm is LANDED too** — CLAMP path (where both traps are observable), byte-identity vs `bicore`, plus a **discrimination gate** that FAILS if the GPU's output is identical to the *nearest* reference everywhere, because otherwise a kernel that silently dispatched the rung-13 blob would score a perfect green. ⭐ And a pre-burn finding that makes the burn interpretable: **bilinear reads 8 quotient bits nothing had ever compared** (nearest consumes only `uv >>> 16`, so 17/17 proves the reciprocal only in the high half). `texgate` **gate 7** now compares the full 16.16 word — **5140 quotients, ZERO differ** — and **gate 8** proves it connected (the correction step moves **3151** quotients, against the **186 texels** gate 6 measured: **17× more error hidden by nearest than exposed by it**). ⇒ **A red bilinear burn is the BLEND, not the divider.** Gates 9/10 give the new four-tap addressing its first oracle (2178 samples, WRAP+CLAMP; corner-exact at all 64 centres). `burn-prep` refuses to flash a `gputex` lacking the arm or its discrimination check. ⛔⛔ **THE BURN RAN 2026-07-28 AND FOUND A REAL DEFECT THROUGH A GREEN RESULT.** `gputex` said **BILINEAR 5 of 5 EXACT** and the filter was still wrong: frame 0 (1:1 identity) reported `vs NEAREST: 35 px differ`, **falsifying pre-registered prediction 3**. Bilinear at exact 1:1 magnification must reproduce the texture EXACTLY. Taps were `floor(u)`/`frac(u)`; they must be **`floor(u-0.5)`/`frac(u-0.5)`**. ⭐ **CONVENTION-FREE**: for texel *i*'s centre at *i+c*, correct nearest = `floor(u-c+0.5)` and correct linear = `floor(u-c)` — differing by **exactly 0.5 for every c**; AGNOS shipped both as `floor(u)`. Not a matched pair under ANY convention, and nearest is pinned 17/17, so linear moved. Adjudicated by 4 independent analyses + 3 adversarial refuters, **0 of 3 refuted**. ✅ **FIXED in-cycle**: `v_add_u32 v23, 0xFFFF8000, v23` per axis (580 → **584 dwords**, no new VGPR), `BI_HALF` in bicore, matching bias in texcore + bimodel. ⛔ NOT folded into `gpu_tex_prep`'s `mu`/`mv` — `limu` is derived from `mu`, so it would shift the out-of-domain predicate half a texel. ⛔⛔ **THE LESSON: byte-identity between shader and reference is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to a SHARED convention error.** All four implementations carried it, so all agreed and every gate went green — and `bigate` G2 / `texgate` GATE 10a probed **exact integers**, which `tex_uv_at` (always +32768 for the pixel centre) **can never emit at any integer scale**: evidence collected on the null set of the error, with 10a *asserting* the bug. Only the **discrimination gate** caught it — the one measurement not compared against the reference. ⇒ **At least one gate must test an EXTERNAL invariant.** New: `texgate` **GATE 11** (absolute: bilinear at 1:1 == the source texture, via `tex_uv_at`) + **GATE 12** (falsifies it, 63/64); GATE 10 inverted to texel centres/boundaries; `bigate` G2 re-anchored + G2b; `bimodel` **M6**; `gputex` asserts frame 0 == 0, `burn-prep` enforces it. ✅✅ **RE-BURN 2026-07-28 (`tex2.txt`): RUNG 15 CLOSED AND IRON-VALIDATED — all four predictions hold.** `gputri --cov` 20/20; `gputex` exit 95, BILINEAR **5 of 5 EXACT**, **frame 0 `vs NEAREST: 0`** (was 35) — the falsified prediction now CONFIRMED. ⭐ `exit 95` IS the assertion passing (the 1:1 gate returns 86 on one differing pixel), so green is now positive evidence rather than absence of evidence. Golden data moved on all five frames as predicted (751 → **680** total) because the reference moved with the shader; frame 0 is the only number that could adjudicate, because it is pinned by an EXTERNAL invariant rather than by agreement. | 1 | 1.56.26 |

| **1.56.32** ▶ **CONSUMER SHIPPED, PRE-REGISTERED, NOT YET FLASHED** | **Rung 19 `pilot` — THE CONSUMER CLOSE.** [`cyrius-mine-cart`](https://github.com/MacCracken/cyrius-mine-cart) **0.1.0** — a ride through a curving mine tunnel, textured and perspective-correct, drawn entirely by `#92` op `0x0F`. **The first thing outside this repo's test tree to use the 3D ops at all.** ⭐ **This cut pays the rung-closure debt.** Rule 2 says the kernel is never more than one rung ahead of a shipped consumer; **all nine ops `0x08`–`0x10` had shipped with no consumer**, every one of them closing on an instrument, which rule 1 explicitly forbids as a closure condition. **No kernel code changed** — the consumer is a sibling repo and the kernel's contribution is a staging row plus the rubric. That is what "closes on a shipped consumer release" should look like: had the kernel needed changing, the ABI was not finished in 1.56.31. **Frame path:** `#86` ×5 slots (1.16 MB) → `#72` → **ONE `#92` dispatch carrying two op `0x0F` records** → `#90` readback → `#73` → `#39` blit. 640×400, 66 tris/frame, two 128×128 procedural textures, 8 segments far-to-near (op `0x0F` REPLACES and a tunnel is convex from inside, so painter's order **is** the depth test — no z-buffer). ⚠ **DEVIATION FROM THIS ROW AS WRITTEN: fullscreen `#39`, not a setu window via `#84`.** Op `0x0F` draws into the kernel's shared blit back buffer (`gpu_bb_a_mc`/`_b_mc`), so a windowed client and the compositor would contend for the same surface; the windowed seam needs that resolved and is not free. Fullscreen is the honest first consumer. ✅ **Host gate (`--check`, runs on iron, needs no GPU), 300 scripted frames, every record re-parsed FROM ITS PACKED BYTES** rather than from the emitter's inputs: 66/66 tris on every ride frame, **0** pixel-losing drops, peak `\|D\|` **260,066,240** of 2,147,483,647 (**8×** margin), worst ride frame **15,660/16,000** coverage samples. ⭐ **Two assertions that are NOT ABI rules**: a coverage sweep (legal-and-empty is the one failure a burn cannot distinguish from a refused batch) and a **mutation** setting one fraction bit in an accepted record, which the gate must catch and name `GPO_E_SUBPIXEL` — a gate that cannot fail has not passed. ⛔ **Two gate defects found by reading its own numbers, not by it failing**: the coverage check **summed two overlapping lists** (19,440 of 16,000 possible — a count above its own denominator passes any threshold) **and counted the full-screen background quad**, so it answered "yes" for a frame where every tunnel triangle had collapsed; and the drop counters were **per-frame, read once**, reporting "0 dropped" while 26 triangles had gone missing. ⚠ Also corrected: the frustum ran **8:1** when `w ∈ [256,4096]` with `w ∝ depth` makes **16:1** the hard ceiling — geometry nearer than the near plane is not drawn, which is exactly what should fill the screen near a wall. Coverage 41% → 98%. Validated byte-identical under mirshi as an `--agnos` binary and loaded+run in ring 3 on the real kernel in QEMU (the GPU path stays iron-only: `#86` slots come from the carveout). Tracker: [`#tracker-15632-mine-cart`](https://github.com/MacCracken/agnosticos/blob/main/docs/development/iron-nuc-zen-log.md#tracker-15632-mine-cart) — **the first GPU burn in this arc whose primary oracle is a person looking at the screen**, hence a shell command and never a boot selftest. | 1-2 | 1.56.29 |
| **1.56.33** | **MODESET — the COLD case, item 7's true residual.** The live-pipe half is DONE (see Where it stands). > ### ⭐ START HERE — updated 2026-07-27, kernel 1.56.23
>
> **The 3D rung ladder is the live thrust, and it is at 14b.** Rungs 9–14 are **correctness-closed on
> iron**: edge coverage · attribute interpolation · tri-list · texturing (17/17 byte-identical, both
> formats, WRAP, FULLCOV) · **op 0x0C `TEX_LIST` fused dispatch (1.56.23, burn 1, exit 95)**.
>
> **⭐⭐⭐ RUNG 14b IS CLOSED (1.56.23, burn 2, exit 95).** `GPU_TEX_FLAG_COLMAJOR` renders
> byte-identical to row-major on all three arms across two boots, and the op-to-op seam closed with
> it. ⛔⛔ **The 177 ns/LAUNCHED-wave figure this row used to cite is RETRACTED** — six RM/CM pairs
> fit **−5 ns/launched (zero) · 36 ns/WORKING · 264 µs fixed**, i.e. an exiting wave is FREE and the
> original two-point fit had no constant term to put the fixed cost in. A 640-column frame is
> **4.6 ms row-major** (not 24.5) and **0.09 ms col-major**. Col-major is kept because it is 50×
> cheaper, NOT because row-major is impossible.
>
> **✅ `F` IS ANSWERED (iron 2026-07-28): PER-PRIMITIVE, 7.35 µs each, and it is ALL in `gpu_texl_build`
> / `gpu_tex_prep` — `build` is 117× the `validate` slope.** A 640-column DOOM frame carries **4.71 ms
> of CPU**, 16.5% of a 28.6 ms budget, and no transpose removes any of it.
>
> ⛔⛔ **THIS RE-RANKS THE WHOLE 3D LANE. The GPU is no longer the bottleneck of a DOOM frame — the CPU
> prep is, by 52×.** Frame totals: row-major 4.71 CPU + 4.60 GPU = **9.31 ms**; col-major 4.71 + 0.09 =
> **4.80 ms**. Rung 14b's 50× is real but GPU-only, so at the frame level it is **~1.9×**. Col-major
> stays; it is simply no longer where the time is.
>
> **NEXT BITE: `gpu_tex_prep`.** 7.35 µs per primitive is ~22,000 cycles at 3 GHz to prepare one affine
> frame — far too many for the arithmetic involved, which makes it a defect-shaped cost (a division, a
> loop, or a memory round-trip) rather than an irreducible one. Read it before optimising it, and
> measure with the profile that now exists rather than re-deriving a model.
>
> ⛔ **Two things this cycle overturned that are easy to re-assume:**
> 1. **Rung 14 is DISPATCH-bound, not bandwidth-bound** (52.7 µs fixed vs ≥3680 MB/s measured).
> 2. **"Walls batch as textured quads" is REFUTED** — `tests/gpu/doomwall.cyr` measures 4096/4096 px
>    wrong at a 1.5× depth ratio, because `ty_step = 1/depth` is a hyperbola in screen x. **Batch by
>    DISPATCH, never by geometry.** A single column IS bit-exactly an op 0x0B quad (`doomcol`, 7/7).
> 3. **And one the burn itself overturned:** fusion removes **submission** cost, not **shading** cost.
>    Both the fused and the unfused path launch the same wavefronts. Measured 2.78×, not the
>    pre-registered >10×.
>
> ⚠ **Op-code space:** `GPU_OP_SUPPORTED = 0x1F1F` (0x00–0x04, 0x08–0x0C). `0x0C` is **TEX_LIST**;
> the depth-clear row was renumbered to `0x0D`. Verify against `gpo_field_mask` in
> `kernel/core/syscall.cyr`, not against this document.
>
> ⚠ **Shader-copy rule:** `tex_list.s` is `tex_rgba.s`'s body under a new prologue, gated in TWO
> stages by `scripts/check/texl-body-identity.sh` — source text AND **417 shipped dwords**. A fix
> belongs in BOTH files; a col-major variant should be a **third blob**, never an edit to either.

**Where the GPU arc stands (2026-07-28, re-derived from source):** compute (1.54.x), display (1.55.x),
shader compositing, the ML crown, **the live-pipe modeset (1.56.9-1.56.15)** and **a working rasteriser
through rung 14b + 16 (1.56.16-1.56.23)** are all shipped and iron-proven. The ring-3 band is **`#82`-`#94`**
— twelve GPU numbers, **not** the "contiguous `#82`-`#92`" this paragraph claimed; `#93 gpu_modeset_op` and
`#94 gpu_recover_op` were missing from every doc that described the band.

**Start at cut 1.56.24.** The first two cuts cost **zero burns** and are the highest-value work available:
they make the ledger true and fix four real correctness defects (an ABI that accepts two things it cannot
execute, a ring append that can write past its buffer, an off-by-one guard, a missing WRITABLE check). Only
then does building on the record make sense.

⚠ **Read before writing any code:** the "Iron-proven facts" section, "⛔ Falsified — do not re-open", and
"Standing iron hazards" — but **verify each against source first.** The review that produced this plan found
those sections carry stale claims too, including a `phyid` value this file states three times and contradicts
once. Where this document and the kernel disagree, **the kernel is right** and the document gets fixed in the
same change.

**How to work (the discipline that made the last arcs land clean):**
1. **This file is THE single GPU document** — no new GPU arc docs, everything goes here (rule at the top). Live
   state pointer: agnos `docs/development/state.md`. Per-burn CONFIRM/FALSIFY tracker: agnosticos
   `docs/development/iron-nuc-zen-log.md` (newest `#tracker-*`).
2. **Every bite gets a CONFIRM/FALSIFY rubric in the tracker BEFORE the operator flashes.** Burns block the
   operator's only dev machine — engineer the negative outcome to name its own cause (the `#90`/`#91` walk was
   84→76→51→95→95, each burn decisive). One item per burn.
3. ⚠ **RE-ANCHOR EVERY `gpu.cyr` LINE CITATION BEFORE YOU EDIT.** Every `gpu.cyr:NNNN` in the modeset ladder
   and the `MD-` table below was written against an older file and is uniformly **~60 lines short** (e.g.
   `gpu_dcn_write_committed` cited 4977 → really 5037 before H0, 5054 after). The code and the described
   behaviour are correct; only the coordinates rot. `atom.cyr` anchors are exact. Grep for the `fn` name,
   never trust the number. H0's row carries a freshly-verified anchor list.
4. **Verify assumptions at the source, don't trust the map.** The two cleanest wins came from reading the
   actual code before coding: `f64v_fmadd` = SSE2 `mulpd+addpd` (unfused, so f64 GPU == CPU bit-exact) and the
   `#82` MAC = `v_mul_lo_u32`+`v_add_u32` (two's-complement, so signed integers work). Both would have been
   wrong guesses.
5. **Build/flash mechanics (all Claude's, operator only flashes):** `scripts/burn/burn-prep.sh` (sweep + build +
   stamp) → `scripts/burn/burn-verify.sh` → operator runs `install-media.sh --update` (kernel) or `--update-fs`
   (agnos-fs `/bin` only) or `--update-all`. Ring-3 proof binaries stage via `scripts/burn/stage-tools.sh`.
   ⚠ **`MD-4` ratified `--update-all` for every burn carrying `/bin/modeset`** — `--update` is ESP-only and
   has already failed to carry `/bin` twice (risk R11).
6. **The ML follow-on (wiring the offload into the full forward) is a CONSUMER task, not a kernel one** — the
   reusable recipe: add a `*_gpu` tiling peer of the repo's matmul (`#82` int / `#83` f64) with a CPU
   fallback + a `programs/*.cyr` exit-code proof (95 GPU-bit-identical / 96 host-plumbing / 90 mismatch),
   host-prove the tiling (96), stage `/bin/<x>`, `--update-fs`, one burn. ⚠ Cross-repo gotcha: a consumer
   using a NEW lib symbol needs the lib **tagged + pushed** and the dep-tag bumped, or CI resolves the old pin.
7. **The operator (Robert) owns ALL git.** New repos = `mkdir`+`Write`. Cyrius the compiler is hands-off
   except filing issues under `docs/development/issues/` — the language agent owns wrapper/lib work.

The full ladder, oracles, risk register and open decisions for this release are in the sections that follow
the reference material below. They are kept because they were worked out in detail and are ready to execute;
the bite IDs in them (`H0`-`H8`, `L1`-`L2`, `M1`-`M9`) are the **last** use of that convention — read them as
a work list for one release, not as an arc.
