## 8. DECISIONS — `TD-*`, needing an operator call first

⚠ **These `TD-N` are LOCAL to the 3D / raster work (REMAINING item 9). They are NOT the shader arc's `D-1`/`D-2`/`D-4` and NOT the modeset's `MD-*` — both of those are done. Different decisions, kept `TD-` prefixed so the three never get confused again.**

| # | Decision | Blocks | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TD-0** | **Does this plan live in gpu.md as row 9's section, and does `roadmap.md:131` get re-pointed?** This file says both *"the successor doc is now justified"* and *"No successor doc"*, and rule 1 forbids new GPU docs. | the plan's home; every CHANGELOG reference | **gpu.md, here.** Rule 1 wins; the "successor doc justified" line predates its restatement. Fix `roadmap.md:131` — it was written before the 2026-07-23 consumer correction and is stale. ⚠ Cost: gpu.md goes past ~1000 lines and its acknowledged `gpu.cyr:NNNN` staleness worsens. |
| **TD-1** | **Compute rasteriser as the load-bearing mechanism, GFX ring behind a measured gate?** | the entire ladder | **Yes.** Rung 1 of a GFX ladder stands on ~879 unmeasured clear-state registers that are probably not even readable; rung 9 here stands on S3/S4/S5/S6, all iron-attested. And **the precedent is agnos's own**: bite P5 said "**GFX-ring** 2D acceleration" and what shipped at 1.55.30 was `DMA_DATA` on the existing MEC ring. The GFX prerequisite was imaginary one level down, once already. |
| **TD-2** | **Does row 9's "3D" mean *a rendering primitive with a caller*, or specifically *the fixed-function GFX pipeline*?** | whether Phase III is a probe or an arc | **A rendering primitive.** But this is a **substitution** and I am flagging it rather than making it quietly — `feedback_dont_dodge_large_items` is exactly this failure mode. If the answer is "the fixed-function pipeline", this ladder is wrong and the GFX arc opens with **rung 5 (recovery) as its rung 1 regardless**. |
| **TD-3** | **Kernel-owned render arena with opaque handles at `0xB0000000` (256 MB, ending at the carveout top), or multi-page `#86` slots?** | rungs 11+; **nothing above 800×600 works without an answer** | **Arena.** Multi-page slots leak a size class into the ABI and re-open the "no MC address crosses ring 3" property. The top-of-carveout placement is deliberate: an overrun runs off the end into the fault net, not into the console FB or the TMR. **Gated on rung 6's read-only verification** — with no page tables, "free" must be measured. |
| **TD-4** | **Vertex transform on the CPU in ring 3, or a GPU vertex stage?** | rungs 13–19; the `#92` record shape | **CPU, in ring 3.** The kernel receives screen-space vertices with `1/w`. Keeps every kernel shader branch-free and fixed-trip-count, which is what keeps the runaway-wave class eliminated. ⚠ Honest narrowing: the delivered pipe therefore has **no vertex stage**. Revisit only if the pilot is vertex-bound, which at a few hundred triangles it will not be. |
| **TD-5** | **Depth by per-tile serialisation (one wave owns a tile, triangles in submission order), or atomics/binning?** | rung 17 | **Per-tile serialisation for v1.** No atomics, no binning pass, and order-independence is *directly* testable. agnos has never tested gfx90c atomics from a raw non-HWS HQD queue. If rung 17's both-orders oracle fails, the atomics variant is a separate, larger bite — **and rung 16 exists so that failure is not misattributed to binning.** |
| **TD-6** | **Op codes `0x08`–`0x0C`, leaving `0x05`–`0x07` reserved as declared, and mint the second `#89` mask word now?** | rung 9; ABI stability | **Yes to both.** Squatting on `0x05` would make the support mask a lie. Minting `+32` costs nothing today and is impossible to do cleanly after four more codes are gone. |
| **TD-7** | **DOOM's colormap: a DOOM-shaped kernel op, or a general IDX8+LUT format flag on `0x0B`?** | rung 14, and kernel-growth doctrine | **A format flag.** `project_agnos_kernel_growth_rules` — the kernel grows per native workload, never to chase a consumer's internals. "Indexed source + LUT" is a general graphics format and it preserves the pixel-identical oracle exactly; a `SPAN_DOOM` op would not. |
| **TD-8** | **Does Phase III open at all, and on what trigger?** | G1–G3; 15–25 additional burns | **Not now. Open only on a written, measured, three-part trigger, all three required:** (i) the pilot is measured **fill-rate-bound below 30 fps** at the target resolution, **and** (ii) rung 1 proved context registers are readable (so G3's preamble has an oracle), **and** (iii) rung 5 arm D showed the console survives a GC stall. ⚠ **Hold me to this row.** If the pilot is *not* fill-rate-bound and Phase III still stays shut, that is correct; if it *is* and Phase III stays shut, that is me dodging and should be called out. |
| **TD-9** | **The missing 3D math (4×4 / vec3): a module inside `cyrius-mine-cart`, or a new library repo?** | rung 19 | **A module inside the pilot first; promote to a repo only when a second consumer appears.** Scaffolding a library speculatively is the same anti-pattern one level down. ⚠ If the operator wants the repo now, the name is a naming-lane call (`feedback_naming_lanes`: system libs Sanskrit/Hindi) and I will not pick it. |
| **TD-10** | **Does rung 9 open if rung 10's projected crossover, or rung 8's sadish-agreement, comes back bad?** | rungs 9–12 | **Pre-committed answers, so the trap cannot arrive as a surprise:** if rung 8 cannot achieve byte-identity with sadish, the arc's premier oracle degrades to ±1 coverage step and **that is reported before rung 9**. If rung 10's batched crossover sits above a real frame's coverage, **rungs 11–12 do not open on the coverage justification** and the arc re-bases on rung 14. |
| **TD-11** | **Which release does this arc ship in?** Memory says GPU is ONE release (1.56.x); Phase 0+I+II is materially larger than a patch series. | every CHANGELOG line; the release table | **Operator's call — I will not name a number** (`feedback_no_unprompted_version_bumps`: bump on cycle OPEN, operator tags on CLOSE). Note only that Phase 0+I+II is ~19 items and 5 nominal flashes, which is minor-sized. One patch per rung, per rule 3. |
| **TD-12** | **May mabda's PM4 / register tables be vendored into the agnos kernel, or must they be re-derived?** | G1–G3 only | **Vendor — with all three Cyrius port hazards in §3 explicitly checked** and every symbol prefixed. Re-deriving 97 register addresses by hand is how transcription errors enter; the diff against `radv-triangle.ib.txt` is a stronger check than re-derivation anyway. |

## 9. DEFERRED, WITH REASONS

| Deferred | Why | Re-open trigger |
|---|---|---|
| **The GFX ring as a capability** (G1 is a one-burn *decision*) | Not size — **recoverability**. Non-local failure modes (a bad EXP presents as a VGT stall; unset `CB_COLOR0_ATTRIB2` presents as a healthy ring drawing nothing), ~879 unmeasured clear-state registers, probably no read-back oracle, on the box that is also the console. On Linux each of mabda's ten catalogued causes was a 2-second TDR and a retry; here each is a dead box and a lost burn. | **TD-8**'s three-part trigger |
| **MIMG / `image_sample` / T# / S#** | The ALU gather path (4 × `global_load_dword` + `v_cvt_f32_ubyte0..3` + lerp + `v_cvt_pk_u8_f32`) uses **only iron-proven instructions**, needs no descriptors, no tiling, no format enums. And S12 measured this path **memory-bound at ~87 % fixed cost**, so manual filtering is close to free. MIMG is a *performance* bite with a working fallback. ⚠ And gpu.md's own "free ten-minute llvm-mc check" has **no valid oracle** (§6). | sampling is >30 % of a **measured** frame profile |
| **Perspective-correct interpolation before rung 18** | `v_rcp_f32` adds a rounding site, and a bit-exact oracle is what makes rungs 9–14 cheap. It lands where a mesh justifies it. **And it is not needed for DOOM at all** — DOOM's texturing is affine per primitive (verified, §4 rung 14). | rung 18 |
| **Near-plane clipping** | Rejecting any triangle with a vertex behind the eye is correct-but-conservative and costs nothing. | a consumer that needs real clipping |
| **MSAA · mipmaps · cubemaps · anisotropy · stencil · blend modes beyond premultiplied src-over** | None has a caller. mabda's own native 3D is a fullscreen triangle with none of them. 2D gets analytic AA free from op `0x08`; 3D edges can supersample in-shader. | a consumer that shows the artifact on the panel |
| **Tiled / DCC surfaces** | Linear render targets sidestep `gfx_v9_0_tiling_mode_table_init` and `GB_ADDR_CONFIG` entirely. And SDMA — whose hardware does the swizzle — **does not fetch on this box** after five burns. mabda's `COPY_TILED_SUB_WINDOW` builder exists if this ever matters. | a measured bandwidth wall traced to linear access |
| **A GL / Vulkan / WebGPU surface, and the soorat / kiran ports** | Standing 1.54.x non-goal. soorat is Rust + wgpu + WGSL against a WebGPU-shaped device; **what seam it wants is a soorat/kiran decision, not a kernel one** — which is ordering candidate (a) from this file's own 3D section, and still the only one that produces evidence. | the port itself, telling us |
| **An in-kernel SPIR-V compiler** | Still **KILLED under D-1**: +4,641 LOC, no SPIR-V producer in the ecosystem, host-side offline emission is the settled path. Rung 7 keeps it host-side. | never, absent a SPIR-V producer |
| **A gfx90c instruction emulator for host-side shader testing** | Attractive — macoy.me and Collabora both name a simulator as decisive — but it is mabda-scale work, and rungs 2/7/8 already move the encoding, the algorithm and the oracle off iron. | rungs 9 + 17 together cost more than 4 burns |
| **SDMA** | Ring-up verified, **five burns of no fetch**. Not the 2D primitive, not the 3D primitive. | — |
| **Hardware cursor · DCN second plane · native-res console (MD-9)** | Display lane, each with a written reason. ⚠ MD-9 becomes worth re-opening **after rung 13** — DOOM-at-native is the caller MD-9 lacked when it was declined, and per `gpu.md:796` it needs **no modeset** (the link is already 2560×1440; only the *surface* is 800×600 under DSCL). | rung 13's measured GB/s |

**Not deferred, and it closes an old loop:** `[[project_gpu_arc_close_iam_gpu_display]]` triggers when REMAINING empties. **Row 9 is the last row**, so this arc's close carries the `iam`/`mihi` sovereign GPU-detection follow-on and the `ai-hwaccel` `readlink#89` → `sys_readlink#70` fix with it.

**Count flashes, not rungs.** 22 named items; **5 nominal flashes**.

Plus arm D (does the console survive a GC stall — the never-measured assumption under the entire GFX risk model) and arm E (S6's guard finally *measured*) | 5 | **rung 6** `arena-audit` (read-only) |
| **3** | The rasteriser exists and is byte-identical to the reference; **the kill gate fires with a pre-registered number**; interpolated attributes | 9 · 10 · 11 | — |
| **4** | GPU coverage in a shipped `crab`/`jalwa` window; nearest-neighbour texturing with the 1:1 absolute oracle; **DOOM's world at 800×600** | 12 · 13 · 14 | — |
| **5** | Bilinear; depth proven by order-independence; perspective; **the pilot on the panel** | 15 · 17 · 18 · 19 | — |
| *(gated)* | The GFX-ring **decision**, not a capability | G1 (+G2, G3 only if G1 and TD-8 both say go) | — |

**Zero-burn work:** rungs 2, 3, 7, 8, 16 — the PM4 validator, the relocation manifest (on Linux, using mabda's existing dumper), the assembler agreement, the CPU reference, the tile-ownership assertion. **~500–700 lines that touch no display register and retire the two failure classes that have cost this arc most: an oracle that cannot distinguish outcomes, and a ring program that does not do what its comment says.**

> **⚠ Read 9–12 as the real number and 5 as the optimistic branch.** The record is decisive: the D lane spent **5 flashes for 1 result**; the M lane's honest budget was *"expect three, budget two to three more."* Rungs 9, 14 and 17 are each individually likely to cost 2. Phase III's 15–25 is a reduction claimed on the strength of instruments that do not exist yet and is **the least-evidenced number in this section.**
>
> **What makes the rung-per-flash cadence affordable at all** is the H3/M4–M8 economy: rungs 9–19 are `--flags` on **one re-runnable ring-3 tool** (`/bin/gputri`), so once the kernel is on disk each rung is a command at the prompt, not another flash. Every kernel shader is behind an idempotent `*_arm()` residency latch, so an unexercised op arms nothing and cannot affect boot — which is what makes it safe to land three shaders per flash.

---

## 11. RISKS

### Risk register — how this blanks or wedges the box

| # | How | Cause | Operator sees | Log survives? | Recovery | Reflash? | Mitigated by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GPU stops responding; shell alive | runaway or stalled wave on the MEC ring | `exit 91`, forensic block, bracket names the packet | **YES** — `klug_spill()` on every timeout | requeue (R-2/R-3), else `gpu_wedged` + CPU compositor fallback | **No** | rungs 4, 5 |
| 2 | Whole box hangs, console frozen | CPU spins unbounded on a GPU condition | dead box | ⚠ only if spilled **before** the submit | power cycle | No | every wait bounded by `gpu_wait_done`; H1-style pre-spill before each risky submit |
| 3 | Console garbage or wrong colours | a raster kernel store lands in the **console FB at carveout offset 0** — **there is no GPU-side isolation, `VM_CONTEXT0` is disabled, and an out-of-bounds store lands somewhere REAL** | corrupted screen | YES | reboot | No | in-kernel bbox validate+clamp (rung 12) **+ the measured** EXEC guard (rung 5 arm E) **+** arena at the top of the carveout so overruns exit into the fault net |
| 4 | PSP refuses firmware on the next boot | a stray write into the **TMR at `0x60000000`** | boot-time GPU failure | YES | reboot; TMR is re-set up at boot | possibly | same placement rule; `GPU_FB_BACK_LIMIT` already exists and the arena is declared against it; rung 6 verifies |
| 5 | Panel black, kernel instruments green | rung 19 present-path bug, or DCN shadow/VUPDATE | black screen, green log | YES | reboot | No | **two independent oracles at rung 19** (eye **and** `#90` capture) so the negative branch names the half. ⚠ `OPTC0_UNDERFLOW` bit10 is **sticky and already 1 at boot** — delta checks only; I1/I2 free-run and are blind to the picture |
| 6 | GFX ring wedges the GC block | any of mabda's ten catalogued TDR causes | `exit 91`, bracket names the packet | YES | G2's recovery; else power cycle | No | rungs 2, 4, G2, and **replay-before-compose** |
| 7 | Console blanks on a GC wedge | GC and DCN more coupled than assumed | black screen | ⚠ only via pre-spill | power cycle | No | **rung 5 arm D measures this for free, at flash 2, before any GFX code exists** |
| 8 | Silent wrong pixels, no hang | premultiplication violated (`c > a`), or a `CB_COLOR0_ATTRIB2`-class silent drop | washed-out or black output, **no error** | YES | — | No | byte-compare against I7 at **every** rung; I6 assertions |
| 9 | A green gate on a build where the flag did not compile in | `#ifdef` mis-wiring | everything "passes" | YES | — | **yes, a wasted one** | `verify_marker` on a string **inside** the flag's own `#ifdef` **plus** the negative check it is absent from a bare build (`ATOM_DRY` was a silent no-op for two burns) |
| 10 | GC clock/power-gated mid-arc | SMU-driven GFXOFF on an APU; `gpu.cyr:755` already notes a CNTL reading back unchanged means gated | reads return 0 or garbage | YES | reboot | No | the existing readback guard; the forensic block records `GRBM_STATUS` |

### How this arc could fail or mislead — ranked by damage

1. **The tier-1 consumer may evaporate, and rung 0 does not fully protect against it.** S12 measured ~60 µs fixed cost per submission at ~87 % overhead; a ~600 px glyph costs the CPU ~3 µs. **Unbatched, the GPU loses by more than an order of magnitude and it is not close.** The case survives only through frame-level batching — which is precisely why rung 0b *builds that path first*. But rung 0b's batching is over the *existing* ops; whether it also amortises rung 9's **coverage generation** is what rung 10 measures, and rung 10 could say no. Mitigation is real (rung 10 is a kill gate with a pre-registered number, and rung 14 is an independent justification), but **if you read one paragraph adversarially, read this one.**
2. **Byte-identity with sadish may be unachievable at reasonable cost.** sadish *scatters* analytic-x span overlap into a per-row accumulator across sub-scanlines; the per-lane reformulation re-derives the same quantity per pixel. If the fixed-point rounding cannot be made to agree exactly, the arc's premier oracle degrades from *byte-identical* to *±1 coverage step*. Rung 8 finds this at **zero burns** — which is the only reason it is acceptable at all.
3. **Recovery may be worse than the hang.** Arm C (matmul bit-correct before *and* after) catches gross corruption, but only for one workload; a recovery that silently poisoned the resident shader arena would pass it. Mitigation: **`gpu_wedged` (R-4) is the shipped default on any ambiguity — when in doubt, refuse to use a GPU you cannot vouch for.** Deliberately conservative; it will occasionally cost a working GPU to a false positive.
4. **VGPR pressure is the largest unmeasured assumption and there is no oracle for it.** Rung 17 holds colour + depth + three edge accumulators + UV per lane across the triangle loop. If the live count exceeds the `RSRC1` declaration, the wave spills to scratch (`COMPUTE_TMPRING_SIZE`, never used by agnos) or launches at reduced occupancy — and **S1 says none of those registers is GRBM-readable.** The lane-witness counter separates "no wave ran" from "wave ran wrong"; it does not measure spill. **Rung 17 is the rung most likely to cost 2–3 flashes instead of 1.**
5. **Rung 17's grid is an order of magnitude past anything agnos has dispatched.** 8×8 tiles at 800×600 is ~7,500 workgroups. Whether one dispatch at that scale retires inside the 100 ms watchdog is untested, and the failure presents as `GPO_E_DISPATCH` — **indistinguishable from a hung kernel without the bracket.** A second reason rung 4 must precede it.
6. **The CPU reference and the GPU kernel share an author and can agree while both are wrong.** A systematic winding or y-flip error passes both. Rung 8's shared-edge internal-consistency test and rung 13's 1:1 checkerboard (an absolute test against an artifact the reference did not produce) narrow it; they do not close it. Residual, stated.
7. **The bracket is real but can be wide** (§5, I4), and instrumenting can move the bug. Labelled, not eliminated.
8. **Consumer-repo churn is the schedule risk.** Rungs 0, 12, 13, 14, 19 each need a release outside agnos — aethersafha, dhancha, sadish, cyrius-doom, plus a new game — with dep pins. The one-rung-ahead rule is the brake, and it is a **discipline** brake, not a mechanical one. If the cadence cannot be absorbed the kernel gets ahead of its callers by the front door.
9. **This arc walks past mabda's graphics prior art** — the 97-register table, the ~40 TDR-avoiding constants, the four-block clear-triangle stream. That is the most complete, HW-proven, Cyrius-native prior art in the ecosystem, and `feedback_redesign_dont_reinvent` cuts against discarding it. **The defence is recoverability, not size.** It is a judgement call, not a derivation, and it is recoverable later — the table does not rot, and G1 keeps a one-burn door open.
10. **The word "3D" is doing work it must earn.** Rung 13 delivers row 9's literal stated end state (*a textured triangle on the panel*) via a compute rasteriser. If the operator's intent for row 9 is the fixed-function pipeline, this ladder satisfies the letter while substituting the mechanism. **TD-2 exists so that is an operator call and not a quiet dodge** — `feedback_dont_dodge_large_items` says I would otherwise be its next instance.
11. **Solo-developer and single-box risk, inherent and unmitigable.** One dev box, which is also the display, and it is the operator's work machine — every burn blocks his work. Every design choice above (ring-3 tools over boot self-tests, read-only riders, host-first oracles, recovery before rasteriser) is a response to that constraint, and none of them removes it.

---

## 12. DEFINITION OF DONE

1. `#92` ops `0x08`–`0x0C` implemented, each validated-before-dispatch, each advertised in `#89`'s mask (and the second mask word minted).
2. Every Phase II rung byte-identical to the ring-3 CPU reference on iron, `exit 95`.
3. **`#92` ops `0x02` and `0x03`, shipped long ago with no caller, have callers** — and aethersafha's frame contains no full-screen CPU clear and no undamaged full-screen CPU copy.
4. **sadish ships a batched GPU coverage path** and a `crab` or `jalwa` window's fills and glyph edges come off the GPU, pixel-identical to CPU. *(Waived only if rung 10 kills tier-1 coverage — in which case the waiver is written into gpu.md **with the measured number**.)*
5. **cyrius-doom renders its world through the GPU at ≥ 800×600, byte-identical to its own CPU renderer at 320×200.**
6. The pilot renders a depth-tested, perspective-correct, bilinear-textured mesh on the panel, confirmed by the operator's eye **and** a `#90` byte-compare.
7. **A wedged GPU is detected, bracketed to a packet, spilled to `/klug.txt`, and either requeued or degraded with the console and shell alive — proven on iron, not asserted.**
8. Every burn written into `iron-nuc-zen-log.md` with its armed CONFIRM/FALSIFY rubric published **before** the flash.
9. Row 9's REMAINING empty; `roadmap.md:131` fixed; the `iam`/`mihi` GPU-detection follow-on and the `ai-hwaccel` `readlink#89` → `sys_readlink#70` fix filed.

**Explicitly NOT required to close:** one line of GFX-ring code · a `CP_RB0` register · `CLEAR_STATE` or a single context register · MIMG, T#, or S# · MSAA, mipmaps, cubemaps, or stencil · tiled or DCC surfaces · a GL/Vulkan/WebGPU surface · one line of soorat, kiran, or WGSL · a second display plane · MD-9 · SDMA.

## 13. CORRECTIONS TO THE RECORD THIS SECTION MAKES

1. **Row 9's `Depends on: 7` is wrong.** The modeset is a prerequisite for *displaying* on a link the firmware did not light, **not** for *rendering*. `#90 gpu_readback_shm` is implemented and iron-proven and gives a programmatic pixel oracle with the display untouched; Fuchsia's `vkreadback` and mabda's own e2e both validate a first render with no display at all. Re-type as `depends on: nothing; MD-9 for the native-res rungs only`. (Moot in outcome since item 7 closed 2026-07-24 — load-bearing in burn design, because Phase 0/I/II rungs 9–13 carry **no display blast radius at all**.)
2. **`gpu.md:643`'s "the GPU is also the console" overstates the coupling.** The console is DCN 2.1 — a different IP block, different registers, fetching DCHUB→SDP→data fabric, never through GL2 or GC. A GC wedge *should* leave scanout running. **This assumption underpins the entire GFX risk model and has never been measured.** Rung 5 arm D measures it at zero marginal cost.
3. **The proposed zero-burn `llvm-mc` MIMG bite has no valid oracle** — an assembler validates encoding, not shader-stage legality, and would return a **false green**. See §6 INFERRED.
4. **`gpu.md:200` still calls `#90`/`#91` "RESERVED with declared shapes."** Both were implemented and iron-proven 2026-07-23 via `/bin/gpucopy`, and `gpu.md:59` says so 140 lines earlier. Fix.
5. **`gpu.md:742` records MD-2 as "✅ SETTLED (phyid=0)"** while `gpu.md:789` records it overturned to `phyid=1` on iron 2026-07-24. Same file, contradictory rows. Fix the stale one in place per the MD convention.
6. **`agnos-userland-abi.md` is missing TEN shipped syscalls** — #82, #83, #86, #87, #88, #89, #90, #91, #92, #93 — **including every number this arc extends.** This is a real defect and it should be fixed before rung 9 adds five op codes to an undocumented surface.
7. **`/bin/gpumm` is staged twice** (`stage-tools.sh:96` stages the standalone `gpumm` repo; `:145` stages `tentib/programs/gpumm.cyr` to the same name, and the second wins at 154,384 B). The standalone `#82`/`#83` seam proof is **unreachable on disk**, which will silently invalidate any future "the seam still works" claim that runs `/bin/gpumm`. Rename one or delete the dead stage.
8. **`gpu_regs.cyr:1028` still says `v_fma_f64`** where the shipped kernel uses separate `v_mul_f64` + `v_add_f64`. A fused FMA would have mismatched 29/64 cells. Fix the comment.
9. **`dhancha/cyrius.cyml` still says "emits Wayland client surfaces."** Wayland is dead; it speaks setu.
10. **Comments claiming "the 39-dword ring program" are wrong** — the C2f envelope is 50 dwords and `gpu_matmul_run` is 68. **Count `gpu_ring_put` calls; do not trust the comments.** Rung 2's validator enforces this mechanically.

---

**Before each bite: re-open this section, name the rung and the `TD-` rows that govern it, in the message, before writing code.** Building an option a decision row rejected, across six bites, without re-opening the plan, is the recurrence that has cost the most trust in this project.

## 3D — what is actually true about it (kept: the consumer-stack correction)

⚠⚠ **CORRECTION, 2026-07-23, and it overturns the original justification for this section.** The first draft
of this split asserted that *"3D has no consumer — name it and it dissolves."* **That is false, and it was
produced by a search bounded to the local filesystem.** The 3D consumer stack is named, cataloged, and its
engine has already shipped:

| Repo | State | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **kiran** | **1.0.0 SHIPPED** — in the v1.0+ libs registry (`docs/applications/libs/README.md:104`, "Game engine (ECS, scene hierarchy)") | The game engine. Rust; a Tier-E **Rust→Cyrius port target** (`planning/software-port-path.md:64`) |
| **soorat** | 1.0, Rust, port target | **A dedicated GAME RENDER ENGINE.** Explicitly **re-homed to the games lane** on 2026-07-05 — *"a game render engine, not the desktop path (desktop renders via mabda + sadish/rekha)"* |
| **joshua** | 0.1.0 scaffold | Game manager + AI simulation runtime |
| **salai** | 0.1.0 | Game editor (visual, for kiran) |
| **impetus** · **raasta** | — | Physics · pathfinding |
| **bsp** | **Ported** to Cyrius already | BSP geometry library |
| **cyrius-mine-cart** | cataloged 2026-04-27, not scaffolded | **The first 3D pilot, deliberately scoped simple** — rail-constrained motion chosen as *"the gentlest first-3D-load on kiran + joshua"* |
| **cyrius-block-game** | cataloged, Path A endorsed 2026-05-22 | Voxel/block-survival, 3D lane |

`planning/shared-crates.md:103` states it outright: **"the 3D lane waits on kiran/joshua."**

**Why the original claim was wrong, recorded so the error is not repeated:** the search read local repos plus
the desktop/ML consumers and concluded no consumer existed. `kiran` is **not checked out locally** — it lives
on GitHub and appears only in the registries. Per [[feedback_meta_repo_know_the_ecosystem]] the registries
(`docs/applications/libs/README.md`, `planning/shared-crates.md`) are authoritative for what exists; a
filesystem listing is not. **An absent directory is not an absent project.**

**What survives the correction, and what does not.**

- ❌ **DEAD: "3D has no consumer, do not open until an application is named."** Two applications are named,
  the engine is at 1.0.0, and a purpose-built game *render* engine exists alongside it.
- ✅ **STILL TRUE: 3D does not belong in THIS arc.** The two halves overlap in zero respects (below), and
  bundling them makes the modeset unclosable.
- ✅ **STILL TRUE: the GFX-ring is not obviously the prerequisite.** The 1.54.x plan's "GFX-ring 2D
  acceleration" shipped as `DMA_DATA` on the existing MEC compute ring. **What kiran/soorat actually need
  from the kernel is an open question that should be answered by reading them, not assumed** — a port of a
  Rust engine may want a very different seam than a from-scratch GFX ring.
- 🔁 **CHANGED: the sequencing question.** It is no longer *"is there a consumer?"* but *"the consumer is a
  PORT TARGET that does not exist in Cyrius yet."* So the ordering candidates are: (a) port kiran/soorat
  first and let the port tell us what kernel seam it needs; (b) build a compute rasteriser now and port onto
  it; (c) open a GFX-ring arc speculatively. **(a) is the cheapest and the only one that produces evidence.**
  ⚠ Note the modeset is a *hard* prerequisite for any of them on a box that cannot cold-boot a display.
- ⚠ **The successor doc is now justified**, where the first draft said not to create it. It should open with
  a read of kiran + soorat, not with kernel code.

---

**3D is not in this arc, and the argument is not size — it is recoverability.**

The two halves of "P6+" overlap in **zero** respects. Different silicon block: DCN 2.1 (DCE base segments,
HUBP/OTG/DIG/DCCG/MPC/AZ) versus GC (CP/SPI/PA/SC/VGT/CB/DB). Different register apertures and BASE_IDX —
with a documented numeric collision between them. Different foundation: the modeset rides `atom.cyr`, which
is **useless** for the graphics pipeline; 3D rides CP/PM4, which is **useless** for modeset. Different oracle:
a photograph and an ear versus a pixel diff. Different forcing function: the modeset has three live,
evidenced justifications; 3D has none.

**The decisive constraint: there is no GPU hang detection or recovery anywhere in agnos.** Every safety
property today is a *CPU-side* bounded watchdog (100 ms, `gpu_wait_done`) plus the `0x1FFC` VM fault net —
both bound how long the **CPU waits**, not what the **GPU does**. A wedged MEC does not stop scanout; that is
the entire reason the compute ring is survivable. **The GFX ring shares the GC block with everything, and the
GPU is also the console.** mabda's Cezanne bring-up notes (`mabda/src/backend_native_pm4.cyr:920-1100`) are a
catalogue of 2-second GFX TDRs — missing `SPI_SHADER_PGM_RSRC3_VS`/`LATE_ALLOC_VS`, missing guard-band
adjusts, `VGT_VERTEX_REUSE_BLOCK_CNTL` zero, binning at the APU default, missing `PFP_SYNC_ME`,
`CB_COLOR0_ATTRIB2` unset (the CB silently drops every pixel). **On Linux each of those was a 2-second
recovery and a retry. On agnos each is a dead box and a lost burn.**

**The size is also real.** A real RADV triangle on this exact Cezanne is **262 register writes** across 81
`SET_CONTEXT_REG` + 11 `SET_SH_REG` + 3 `SET_UCONFIG_REG_INDEX`. agnos's entire proven PM4 vocabulary is
**six packet types** and ~14 register writes per dispatch, with **zero** graphics registers defined anywhere
in the tree (verified by grep for `PA_SC|PA_CL|CB_COLOR|DB_Z_INFO|VGT_|SPI_SHADER|SET_CONTEXT|DRAW_INDEX|
CP_RB0|CLEAR_STATE`). Honest estimate: **25-40 iron bites across 3-4 minor versions**, on a box where the D
lane cost 5 burns for 1 result.

**The precedent is decisive.** Bite P5 of the 1.54.x plan said "**GFX-ring** 2D acceleration". What shipped at
1.55.30 was `PACKET3 DMA_DATA` on the existing MEC compute ring — "the FIRST hardware 2D on sovereign agnos …
no SDMA, no doorbell, no new firmware". **The GFX-ring prerequisite was imaginary one level down.**

⚠ **SUPERSEDED PARAGRAPH — kept only because the survey inside it is still accurate.** Its opening claim,
"3D has no consumer, name it and it dissolves", is **DEAD** — killed by the 2026-07-23 correction at the top
of this section and listed as ❌ DEAD above. **Two applications ARE named: soorat (game render engine) and
kiran (game engine, 1.0.0)**, plus joshua/salai/impetus/raasta and cyrius-mine-cart/cyrius-block-game; an
absent local checkout is not an absent project ([[feedback_meta_repo_know_the_ecosystem]]). What survives
below is the *inventory* — which existing consumers do **not** need 3D — and that part still holds. Read it
as "the shipped desktop/ML stack does not force 3D", never as "nothing wants it".

**~~And 3D has no consumer.~~** ~~Name it and it dissolves:~~ aethersafha/dhancha/setu/rupa is 2D compositing, served
by S2-S12 now; cyrius-doom is a software renderer already presenting tear-free through blit#39; mabda's own
*native* 3D is a fullscreen triangle with no depth, no vertex buffers, no blend, no MSAA; hoosh/attn11/
tentib/prajna are compute. The 1.54.x arc's own Non-goals explicitly exclude a Vulkan/GL API surface. Worse:
**`#92 gpu_shader_op` now HAS a consumer** (aethersafha's compositor, shipped 2026-07-23) — but opening a
3-4-minor GFX programme SPECULATIVELY, before a 3D application drives it, would repeat the C6/`#92` pattern of
building a kernel capability ahead of its caller. [[project_agnos_kernel_growth_rules]] says the kernel grows
per **native workload** — so 3D opens when the soorat/kiran port needs a specific seam, not before.

**If a 3D need ever appears, the answer is a compute rasteriser on the ring agnos already owns.** Everything
it needs is iron-proven: 2-D workgroup grids (`RSRC2 = 0x18E`), 7 kernargs, the `s_and_saveexec_b64` EXEC
bounds guard, per-pixel float ALU with byte↔float converts, a characterised GL2↔scanout coherence model (S3),
and bit-correct blend. Zero new rings, zero new firmware, zero new PM4 classes, **zero GFX-ring wedge
exposure** — a runaway compute wave is bounded by the same fault net and 100 ms watchdog that already govern
every dispatch. Realistic: **4-8 bites, one minor version, most of it host-verifiable against a CPU reference
before any burn** — the opposite of the GFX ring, where nothing can be pre-validated except that it compiles.

**Sequencing recommendation, to be written into the successor doc, not this one:**
1. Any 3D lane opens with a **consumer question**, not code: *name the application.*
2. Its first technical bite is **host-only and takes ten minutes**: `llvm-mc -mcpu=gfx90c` on an
   `image_sample` inside a **compute** kernel. mabda's MIMG encodings (`0xF0800F00 / 0x00400004`, and the
   `da`-bit cube form) are all *fragment* shaders there; if MIMG is compute-legal on gfx9 — it should be,
   but I read encodings, not an ISA doc — **the filtered-texture half of 3D is reachable from the ring agnos
   already owns**, and that materially changes the size of the prize before anyone builds on it.
3. If GFX-ring 3D is ever opened anyway, its ladder **must open with hang detection and recovery, before the
   first GFX fetch** — inverting the usual order, because there is no Linux underneath to reset a wedged ring
   and the operator's only dev box is the display.

**No successor doc.** 3D is row 9 of the release plan above; the consumer is named (soorat/kiran).

## HDMI AUDIO (row 8) — the minimum path, and why the free experiment comes first

**A4 alone does not justify this arc, and the plan should say so out loud.** HDMI audio is a comfort feature
on a box that already has sound out the ALC897 analog jack (1.52.x). And the 07-20 falsification leaves three
A4 candidates — **(a) sequencing, (b) a write that doesn't latch, (c) the bare-metal environment** — of which
**only (a)** is addressed by a modeset. So the odds are roughly even, and **L1 tells you which side you are
on for free, before any burn.**

**The justification is items 2 and 3: agnos cannot display anything it did not inherit.** No cold boot on
firmware that does not hand over a lit pipe, no resolution control ever, no second display, no hotplug — and
the console is an 800×600 surface DCN upscales to 1440p. **Reframe accordingly: A4 is this arc's cheapest
oracle, not its goal.** That reframe survives L1 coming back "sound".

Minimum path, in order, with realistic burn counts calibrated to the record (D lane 5 burns / 1 result;
C2g-1 8 burns; C2f 3):

| # | Bite | Burns | Standalone value if the arc stops here |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L1** | Linux GOP-pipe full-program experiment | **0** | Decisive either way; may retire the whole thesis |
| **H7** | 3-way-anchored register table | **0** | Permanent — every future DCN bite inherits it |
| **H4·H5·H6** | ATOM instrument pack + snapshot DRY + div32 check | **0** | Every future ATOM run becomes readable |
| **H1·H2·H3** | spill · latch · ring-3 tool | **0** (QEMU) | Retires every mandatory-reflash path in the arc |
| **M1·M2·M3** | Read-only dump, one boot, three groups | **1** | The GOP-vs-amdgpu delta at matched mode — a first |
| **MD-2** | ⛔ **THIS ROW WAS WRONG — `phyid = 1`, NOT 0.** It read "✅ SETTLED (phyid=0, object-info-derived)" until 2026-07-28. Overturned on iron 2026-07-24 (M8d/M8e: `phyid=0` wrote the dead instance, `phyid=1` gave reads=21 writes=17 delays=5 clean), fixed at 1.56.14 by re-derivation. See the MD-2 row in the decisions table below, and `gpu.cyr:9208` — all three discovery methods agree on **instance 1**. | **0** | Prevented one wasted destructive burn, then cost trust by staying wrong in two places for four days |
| **M4·M5·M6** | Lock proof · V_TOTAL · same-mode re-commit | **2-3** | **agnos owns the OTG** — item 3 substantially answered |
| **M8·M9** | #4 + #76 inside the envelope, then audio last | **2-4** | The A4 answer, whichever way it falls |

**≈5-9 burns if nothing goes wrong; 10-15 realistically.** M6 is the first genuinely dangerous one, and it is
only survivable because of H3. Note **M7 (`SetPixelClock`) is NOT on the minimum path** — M6 reuses the
running GOP pixel clock deliberately, and the capture shows the OTG edge and the transmitter edge are
separable from the PLL program by ≈16 ms and one DMCUB command. Reprogramming the clock is the step that can
strand the panel; it belongs to the mode-*change* work, not to unparking A4.

⚠ **Two doc blockers must clear before the first bite**, or the next session inherits a dead hypothesis —
exactly the D-7 trap that cost a session on 2026-07-22:
- `agnosticos/docs/development/state.md` still names the **DCCG symbol-clock re-prime** as A4's current lead
  and still says "**THE FULL MODESET IS NO LONGER THE FALLBACK — IT IS THE LEAD**". Both were killed on
  07-19/07-20. Grep it for `241503`, `HDMI_ACR_CTS`, `RDPCS`, or the 105/105 audit: **zero hits**.
- `agnos/docs/development/roadmap.md:144-146` still says "A4 is blocked on the same full-modeset work as P6+,
  and the ATOM interpreter is the lever."

Per [[feedback_read_state_at_session_start]] sessions read `state.md` **first**. Fix both in the same commit
that opens this arc.

## MODESET — decisions needing an operator call first

> ⚠ **These `MD-N` are LOCAL to the modeset work (REMAINING item 7) and are NOT the shader-arc `D-1/D-2/D-4`
> ratified in the release plan above — those are done. Different decisions, kept `MD-` prefixed so the two
> never get confused again.**
>
> **▶ OPERATOR CALL TAKEN 2026-07-23 — `MD-3`, `MD-4`, `MD-5`, `MD-6`, `MD-7`, `MD-8` are RATIFIED as
> recommended.** `MD-1` answered: **start with the harness work list (A), no burns**, ahead of L1 — see the
> L1 correction below. ⛔ **`MD-2` (phyid): this line used to say "SETTLED on paper (phyid=0, UNIPHYA)". IT IS 1, NOT 0** — overturned on iron 2026-07-24, fixed 1.56.14. `phyid` is ATOM #76's parameter and #76 has blanked this display twice; do not take it from paper. No decision now blocks a bite
> (`M8`); it is a paper task, settled off `atom-interp.py` + `M2`, never with a burn. **`MD-9` declined for
> now** — the native-resolution console stays deferred; the operator chose the modeset harness over the
> detour, which is the doc's own recommendation, but note this was a "not this bite", not a permanent no.
>
> ⚠ **CORRECTION TO `MD-1`'s "costs nothing": L1 is zero *agnos* burns but NOT free.** It needs a full
> archaemenid session — root, a pre-reserved 2 MB hugepage, `snd_hda_intel` unbound from `04:00.1`,
> pipewire/wireplumber stopped, raw BAR5 writes — **and Linux booted with amdgpu blacklisted**, which
> invalidates the script's current known-good baseline (captured from an amdgpu-*driven* pipe). It is also
> the operator's machine and Claude cannot run it. Worse, **L1 as specified has no negative control and no
> unguessable-pattern protocol**, violating this file's own rule — so its SILENCE branch is not decisive as
> written. Design the control before booking the session.

| # | Decision | Blocks | Recommendation |
|---|----------|--------|----------------|
| **MD-1** | **When does the modeset work start?** | item 7 | **The consumer-wiring prerequisite is DONE** (release-plan items 1-4, shipped). Remaining before modeset burns: run **L1** (the zero-burn Linux discriminator), which costs nothing and can change the justification. Then start with the harness work list (no burns). Modeset is REMAINING item 7 of the one GPU release — **not** a new minor version. |
| **MD-2** | **phyid — 0 or 1?** | **M8** | **⛔⛔ OVERTURNED ON IRON 2026-07-24 → `phyid = 1`, NOT 0.** ✅ **FIXED 1.56.14 — and the fix is a RE-DERIVATION, not a patched constant:** `gpu_phy_discover()` (gpu.cyr) reads the live DIG **back end** — `DIG_BE_EN_CNTL` bit0 set · `FE_SOURCE_SELECT` ≠ 0 · `DIG_MODE` ∈ {2 DVI, 3 HDMI}, all three required — and `atom_run_transmitter_enable_hdmi()` REFUSES when nothing is live rather than falling back to 0. Flipping the constant to 1 would have left the real defect in place (the arc's most destructive command taking its target from a VBIOS guess). ⛔ **`phyid` (back end / transmitter) is NOT `gpu_audio_dig` (front end / stream encoder)** — DCN assigns them independently; both are 1 here by routing, not identity, and assuming identity is what produced this bug. Host tooling re-derived in lockstep (`atom-interp.py` prefers a snapshot-derived phyid, labels the object-table value `UNRELIABLE`); `mdo_dump` +`M8-digbe` (101 regs) so a capture answers it. **Remaining: re-burn `--dump` to see `phyid = 1` asserted from iron before M8e.** — *the overturning evidence follows* — This row named its own falsification test — *"if PHY0/UNIPHYA reads active-configured, phyid=0 is iron-attested"* — and the M8d dump ran it: **RDPCSTX0 = `0x00D00000` vs RDPCSTX1 = `0x00D1F000`**, i.e. PHY**1** is the configured one, not PHY0. Replaying #76 under the real snapshot seals it: `phyid=0` → `reads=87292 writes=20 delays=43639` **poll storm**, writing `DIG_BE_CNTL` instance **0** (`0x556F`) + RDPCSTX0; `phyid=1` → `reads=21 writes=17 delays=5` **clean**, writing instance **1** (`0x566F`) + RDPCSTX1. The live encoder is instance 1 (`dig1 mode=2` DVI) and instance 0 is OFF (`dig0 mode=1`). With real values the correct target's status bits already read ready — the storm IS the wrong-target signal. Firing #76 at phyid=0 would power-cycle a dead PHY and reprogram a dead encoder while the live link sits untouched. ⚠ The paper derivation below is kept as the SUPERSEDED reasoning: it was flagged best-effort by the tool itself (`atom-interp.py` prints `[TODO confirm on iron]`) and the enum→phyid mapping needs re-deriving, not patching. — *superseded text follows* — **✅ SETTLED ON PAPER 2026-07-24 → phyid=0 (UNIPHYA); iron cross-check rides the M1 dump.** `derive_transmitter_routing` on the real VBIOS (`agnosticos vbios.rom`) is unambiguous: the HDMI path is **connector 0x3113 (HDMI_TYPE_A) → internal encoder enum_id=1 → phyid = enum-1 = 0 (UNIPHYA)**, digfe_sel=0x02 (DIG1). **The "three-way contradiction" was a layer confusion**: `phyid` is the PHY/transmitter *instance* (0 = UNIPHYA), `digfe_sel` is the DIG *front-end* (1). So a phyid=0 transmitter driving a DIG1 picture is the correct FE→BE routing, not a mismatch — which also explains why #76 with phyid=0 touched UNIPHYA/RDPCSTX0 yet the picture is on DIG1 (the DIG1 blanking on that burn was the #76 PHY power-cycle, risk R3, **independent of phyid**). ⚠ **Final iron confirmation is FREE**: M2's RDPCSTX read is in the M1·M2·M3 dump (offsets `0x5D2D`-`0x5D30` = RDPCSTX0) — if PHY0/UNIPHYA reads active-configured, phyid=0 is iron-attested. (A follow-up could add a RDPCSTX1 read to prove PHY1 idle, but the object-info derivation is decisive on its own.) **M8 is no longer blocked on this decision.** — ⛔ *end superseded text. The "follow-up" it waved off is exactly the read that overturned it: RDPCSTX1 is not idle, it is the ACTIVE one. **M8e IS blocked on this decision again.*** |
| **MD-3** | **Scope: same-mode envelope only, or mode change too?** | **M7 · the ladder's length** | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-23. Same-mode only.** M6 reuses the running GOP pixel clock; M7 is built (Python + host diff) but its live rung is **optional and last**. Mode change drags in the scaler (gap 8), the DLG/TTU recompute, and the three literals (gap 9), and the amdgpu trace's HUBP/DLG/TTU values are for a **2-pipe horizontal split** (`RECOUT 0x05a00500` = 1280×1440 per pipe, `MPC_SIZE 0x05a00a00` = 2560) so those deadline numbers are **not reusable** for a single 2560-wide pipe — the trace de-risks the offsets, not the values. Successor: the deferred row of the release plan above — no separate arc doc |
| **MD-4** | **Ring-3 tool vs boot `#ifdef` for the write bites** | **H3 ⇒ every M write bite** | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-23 — the operator approved syscall `#93` AND the `--update-all` flash requirement.** Ring-3, and it is not negotiable. Reflash → power-cycle for every failure mode; N iterations per flash; capture before *and* after; the operator present with the camera up. The D lane's five burns would have been one flash. Cost is one kernel file, one syscall, one tool — QEMU-compilable, iron-neutral. Operator ratifies the new syscall number (**#93**) and the `--update-all` flash requirement |
| **MD-5** | **Does the arc close at the kernel seam or at a consumer?** | scope | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-23. Kernel arc closes at M6 (+ M9 if A4 falls out).** There is no userland modeset consumer yet — `agnodrm`'s DRM/KMS model is the eventual one and is out of tree for this arc. **But do not repeat the C6/`#92` pattern:** if this arc ships a modeset with no caller, the *next* arc's first bite is wiring one, and that must be written into the roadmap at close, not discovered later |
| **MD-6** | **Is `ATOM_HALT` retired?** | H1 · M-lane hygiene | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-23. Retire it once H1 lands.** It is a deliberate unrecoverable hang (IF=0 bare `while`, `main.cyr:1173-1176`) whose only legitimate use is freezing the framebuffer for a photo. With `klug_spill` in place most reasons to reach for it evaporate, and **it must never be paired with a bite whose oracle is a log.** Keep it as a photo tool only, behind its own `verify_marker` row (H8) |
| **MD-7** | **Do the callerless commit primitives get adopted or deleted?** | **M4** | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-23 — and H0's fixes ARE LANDED, so the precondition is met.** `gpu_dcn_write_committed` now takes an explicit `(pipe, off, stride, val)` and indexes the write; the read-back is documented as a shadow read, not evidence. Adopt. `gpu.cyr` (~:5091, re-anchor) says explicitly they exist for "the DCN plane arc" and instructs deleting them if that arc opens without using them. This arc is the caller. ⚠ Adopt **after** fixing the missing pipe stride in `gpu_dcn_write_committed` — it is the same unindexed-write defect the D-lane post-mortem found, latent only because there are no callers |
| **MD-8** | **`gpu_pixclk_discover` runs unconditionally at `main.cyr:1142` and costs ~2 s of every boot.** Diagnostic, or infrastructure? | M5's oracle | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-23 — and `gpu_refresh_measure()` IS MINTED (H0 landed).** Infrastructure — say so. M5 makes PIT-counted refresh the arc's primary oracle (I2), so the measurement stops being a diagnostic. Keep it unconditional, and factor `gpu_refresh_measure()` out of it (H0) so a bite can re-measure without re-reading the totals it just wrote. But **document the boot cost** rather than leaving it unexplained |
| **MD-9** | **Does the arc carry the native-resolution console?** | scope, and it is tempting | **No — and note that it needs no modeset at all.** The link is *already* 2560×1440 at 241.503 MHz; only the **surface** is 800×600 (viewport `0x5EA` = 0x02580320, pitch `0x607` = 832) with DSCL upscaling. A native console needs HUBP viewport/pitch/surface + DSCL bypass + RECOUT + DLG/TTU — **no PLL, no ATOM, no PHY, no OTG disable.** It is the most visible user-facing display win available and it **beats this arc on value per burn**, which is exactly why it must not be smuggled in as a bite. It belongs to the deferred row above; M3 is this arc's only contribution to it (the first scaler read) |

## Risk register

| # | How it blanks / wedges the box | Cause | Operator sees | Log survives? | Recovery | Reflash? | Mitigated by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | Read-only dump touches a power-gated domain | The 2026-07-17 OTG/DCCG read-sweep lock | Frozen console; **last line names the register** | **Yes** — spilled per group | Power-cycle | **No** | H1 · H2 · named-offsets-only rule · progressive print |
| **R2** | A timing write blanks the panel, kernel alive | Wrong offset, or a value the sink cannot lock | Black panel, box up, can type blind | **Yes** — tool ran from the prompt | Power-cycle | **No** | H1 · H2 · H3 · M1's anchors · M5's benign first write |
| **R3** | **ATOM #76 strands the PHY** — *already happened twice* | #76 power-cycles the PHY (556F, 5E03, 5DF0/5DE9 among 17 writes); once accidentally, because `ATOM_DRY` was a flag `build.sh` never emitted | Black panel | **Yes** — pre-state spilled before the call | Frame-count watchdog may relight; else power-cycle | **No** | **All six M8 gates** · H8's `verify_marker` + negative check |
| **R4** | A derived DCN offset written under the OTG lock → HUBP underflow → **black + wedge** | The 0x607 precedent, burn 3 of P4 | Full black, unresponsive, **diagnostic died with it** | No | Power-cycle | **No** (was yes) | H7's ten anchors · anchor-before-write · ⛔ **never bundle a live-pipe write with the diagnostic meant to characterise it** |
| **R5** | Wrong BASE_IDX on a `DOMAIN*_PG_CONFIG` write **power-gates a live pipe** | `0x0080` BASE_IDX 2 collides with `GPU_R_OTG_PIXEL_RATE_CNTL 0x80` BASE_IDX 1; `gpu_reg32` checks nothing | Black panel | Yes | Power-cycle | **No** | Distinct naming (`GPU_R_DCPG_DOMAIN0_PG_CONFIG`) + base asserted in the comment — **naming is the only defence** |
| **R6** | Modeset wedges the kernel outright | A poll that never terminates, or a fault in the write path | Frozen | **Yes** — pre-call spill | Power-cycle | **No** | H1 · H2 · wall-clock bounds everywhere · `power_reset()` as the floor |
| **R7** | Underflow: pipe corrupt but still scanning — a "successful" poke that quietly corrupts scanout | Bandwidth/deadline mismatch after a timing change | Garbled console | Yes | Power-cycle | No | Latch `OPTC0_UNDERFLOW 0x1ACA` bit10 at entry (**sticky**) and check after every write phase |
| **R8** | The OTG lock is left held on an error path | An early return between lock and unlock | Pipe **frozen on its last frame** — which looks exactly like a successful hold | Yes | Power-cycle | No | Unlock on **every** exit path (`gpu.cyr:4926` states it); print `gpu_otg_lock_acked`; report `gpu_dcn_hold_ms`'s actual elapsed |
| **R9** | A burn produces no evidence at all | `ATOM_HALT`'s IF=0 spin: no keyboard, no shell, no capture | Frozen photo | **No** | Power button | **No** (with H2) | MD-6 — retire it as anything but a photo tool; never pair with a log oracle |
| **R10** | A "safe" build is not the build that was flashed | `ATOM_DRY` shipped as a silent no-op for two burns and blacked the display twice | Anything | — | — | Yes | `cmp` **+** `verify_marker` **+** the **negative** check; `burn-prep.sh`'s `env -u` scrub; H8 |
| **R11** | The flash does not carry the tool | `install-media.sh --update` is **ESP-only**; `/bin` needs `--update-all`. Has bitten twice | Tool missing at the prompt | Yes | Re-flash correctly | **Yes** | Every M-lane burn card names `--update-all` explicitly. Per [[feedback_i_build_user_only_flashes]] the operator runs exactly one command |
| **R12** | **The latch itself fails to write** | FS not mounted, or the pre-created file was lost | A persistent bad boot | Maybe | Boot Linux, `--update` | **Yes** | The one remaining reflash path. **H2 must be QEMU-validated (arm → force-fail → confirm skip) before any write bite is flashed** |
| **R13** | An unmeasured "fix" destroys an instrument | The 1.55.20 ramp burn removed the shutdown pop — the arc's only sink-side signal | Nothing, and less than before | Yes | Revert | No | ⛔ No `BURN_AUDIO_TEARDOWN`; measure before and after every change to the audio path |
| **R14** | Nothing is bricked, ever | The VBIOS agnos runs is the **ACPI VFCT copy in RAM**; there is no flash write anywhere in the tree, and UEFI's own display is upstream of everything agnos does | — | — | Full power cycle re-runs AGESA/GOP and relights the panel — **empirically attested twice** | — | *(recorded so the arc does not over-dramatise: the real cost is a mandatory reflash cycle and a lost burn, not an unusable NUC)* |

## MODESET — definition of done

**The arc closes when agnos can take the pipe away from the GOP and give it back.** Concretely, all of:

1. **H0-H8 landed and green on host/QEMU.** Specifically: `klug_spill` proven by mounting the image from the
   host; the arm-once latch proven by a forced failure and a clean second boot; `/bin/modeset` staged and
   answering `--caps`; the ATOM instrument pack re-proving the 1.55.23 DRY trace byte-identically **and**
   returning non-zero on a deliberate desync; snapshot-DRY matching the oracle at a real branch path; the
   div32/mul32 sweep clean; the H7 table reproducing all ten anchors; `verify_marker` rows with negative
   checks for every ATOM and `MODESET_*` flag.
2. **L1 run and its answer recorded in `state.md`**, whichever way it fell.
3. **M1·M2·M3 captured** — the GOP-vs-amdgpu per-register delta at matched resolution, the PHY compared from
   agnos's own side, the scaler read for the first time — all committed to
   `agnosticos/docs/development/prior-art/`.
4. **M5 green:** agnos changed the raster and an **off-GPU instrument** measured the predicted change —
   59.951 Hz → 59.152 Hz, ±200 ppm, three orders of magnitude above the noise floor.
5. **★ M6 green: `OTG_MASTER_EN` 0 → reprogram → 1, with the frame counter observed stopping and resuming,
   the console intact, and underflow still 0.** This is the arc's stated closing condition. **agnos owns the
   OTG.**
6. **`gpu_otg_lock` / `gpu_otg_commit_unlock` / `gpu_dcn_write_committed` have real callers**, with the pipe
   stride fixed — the adopt-or-delete question at `gpu.cyr:5031-5036` answered by adoption.
7. **A4 has an answer, not a park.** Either M9 produces sound, or the arc records — with the modeset edge
   demonstrably performed — that **sequencing is eliminated** and A4's remaining candidates are (b) a write
   that doesn't latch and (c) the bare-metal environment, with L1's result naming the likelier.
8. **Docs true at close:** `state.md`'s A4 paragraph and `roadmap.md` rewritten (MD-1's blockers);
   `roadmap.md:131`'s "P6+ — 3D and full modeset" **split into two rows**, the modeset one closed and the 3D
   one pointing at a successor doc that does not exist yet and should not until a consumer is named; the
   dead `#ifdef HDMI_DCCG` block deleted; the burn ledger in `iron-nuc-zen-log.md` carrying a `#tracker-157x-*`
   section per M bite.
9. **Explicitly NOT required to close:** `SetPixelClock` #12 running live (MD-3); any mode change; the
   native-resolution console (MD-9); EDID/DDC/hotplug; DP anything; one line of 3D.
